Its statement also gives the determinant formula $ad-bc$ for a matrix with rows $(a,b)$ and $(c,d)$.

[F3] The normalized valuation is additive on products, with value one on $\ell$ and value zero on units of $\mathbb Z_\ell$ ([lem-author-d45da46285be-triangular-cosets](item-lem-author-d45da46285be-triangular-cosets)). Determinants multiply, and the positive square root of $\ell$ exists because the real numbers are a complete ordered field and $\ell>0$ ([math:thm-determinant-multiplicative](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-determinant-multiplicative), [math:thm-of-square-roots](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-of-square-roots), [math:cor-cauchy-reals-lub-complete](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/cor-cauchy-reals-lub-complete), [math:def-prime](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-prime)).

[F4] Under countable choice, $G$ is a Hausdorff topological group and $K$ is a compact open subgroup ([lem-author-d45da46285be-triangular-cosets](item-lem-author-d45da46285be-triangular-cosets)). Left translation by a fixed element of a topological group is continuous ([math:lem-topological-group-translations-and-inversion](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-topological-group-translations-and-inversion)). A continuous image of a compact space is compact ([math:thm-compactness-under-continuous-maps](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-compactness-under-continuous-maps)). A subset of $G$ is compact exactly when every cover of it by open subsets of $G$ has a finite subfamily that covers it ([math:lem-compactness-of-a-subspace-is-ambient](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-compactness-of-a-subspace-is-ambient)). A finite list of nonempty sets admits a choice of one element from each set ([math:lem-finite-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-finite-choice)).

[F5] For two-by-two matrices over a commutative ring the product has entries $(AB)_{ik}=a_{i1}b_{1k}+a_{i2}b_{2k}$, and the identity matrix $I$ has diagonal entries one and off-diagonal entries zero ([math:def-ring-matrix-product-identity-and-transpose](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-ring-matrix-product-identity-and-transpose)).

### Proof

**Given:** The local field, subgroup and representation in the statement.

**Goal:** Compute both scalars in the specified normalization.

1.1 For $k\in K$, both $k$ and $k^{-1}$ have entries in $\mathbb Z_\ell$, so their determinants belong to $\mathbb Z_\ell$ by the formula in [F2]. The entries of $I$ give $\det(I)=1$, and multiplicativity gives $\det(k)\det(k^{-1})=1$. Thus $\det(k)$ is a unit of $\mathbb Z_\ell$ and has valuation zero. Every $k_1Dk_2$ with $k_1,k_2\in K$ has integral entries by the product rule, and its determinant has valuation one since $\det(D)=\ell$. [F2, F3, F5]

1.2 The set $KDK$ is invariant under multiplication on either side by $K$ because $K$ is a subgroup. The matrix $\ell I$ is central since $(\ell I)x=\ell x=x(\ell I)$ for every $x\in G$. Thus $(\ell I)K=K(\ell I)K$ and this set is invariant on both sides as well. For each $x\in G$, left translation $g\mapsto xg$ is continuous and maps the compact subset $K$ onto $xK$, so $xK$ is compact. In particular $(\ell I)K$ is compact. [F4, F5, given]

2.1 Conversely, take one of the representatives in [F2]. Put $P=\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\1&0\end{pmatrix}$ and, for $b\in\mathbb Z_\ell$, put $U_b=\begin{pmatrix}1&b\\0&1\end{pmatrix}$. Entrywise multiplication gives $P^2=I$ and $U_b U_{-b}=U_{-b} U_b=I$, so both $P$ and $U_b$ are in $K$. It also gives $PDP=\operatorname{diag}(1,\ell)$ and $U_bD=\begin{pmatrix}\ell&b\\0&1\end{pmatrix}$. Each representative and its full coset for right multiplication by $K$ therefore lie in $KDK$. By step 1.1, the cosets in [F2] give exactly $KDK$. [F1, F2, F5, step 1.1]

3.1 Let $\mathcal U$ be a family of open subsets of $G$ covering $KDK$. It covers each of the cosets in step 2.1, which are compact by step 1.2. By the ambient compactness criterion the set of finite lists from $\mathcal U$ covering each coset is nonempty. Use finite choice to select one such list for each of the $\ell+1$ cosets enumerated in [F2]. Concatenating the lists yields a list whose length is the sum of their lengths and whose members cover $KDK$. The converse direction of the ambient compactness criterion therefore gives compactness of $KDK$. Both indicators are complex-valued functions invariant on both sides under $K$ and zero outside compact sets, so they belong to the spherical Hecke algebra. [F1, F2, F4, step 2.1, step 1.2]

4.1 Each contributing coset has indicator value $1$. The single representative of diagonal valuations $(0,1)$ contributes $\sqrt\ell Y$ to the transform, and the $\ell$ representatives of valuations $(1,0)$ together contribute $\ell\ell^{-1/2}X=\sqrt\ell X$. The indicator of $(\ell I)K$ gives just $XY$, because its support is one coset of diagonal valuations $(1,1)$. Evaluating both transforms at $(u,v)$ gives $\sqrt\ell(u+v)$ and $uv$ on $V^K$. [F1, F2, F3, given, step 2.1, step 3.1] ∎


## Full adelic projective Hamilton group

[Open item](item-def-author-5b8600b4f4af-full-adelic-hamilton-group) · level 4


### Definition

Let $B$ be the rational Hamilton quaternion algebra and $G=PB^\times$ its group of projective units. Write $G_f=G(\mathbb A_f)$ for the finite adelic group and $G_\infty=G(\mathbb R)=B_{\mathbb R}^\times/\mathbb R^\times$ for the real projective group. In the real quotient, two units represent the same element if they differ by a nonzero real scalar. Give $B_{\mathbb R}^\times$ the subspace topology from Euclidean $\mathbb R^4$ in the Hamilton coordinates and give $G_\infty$ the quotient topology.

Let $\mathbb A_f$ be the finite adele ring. The full adele ring is the product ring $\mathbb A=\mathbb R\times\mathbb A_f$, with componentwise operations. Its rational coefficient map sends $a$ to $(\iota_\infty(a),\iota_f(a))$, where $\iota_\infty$ is the canonical real embedding and $\iota_f$ is the diagonal map into the finite adeles.

The projections identify the full adelic group $H=G(\mathbb A)$ with $G_\infty\times G_f$. Give it componentwise multiplication and the product topology. Write $\Gamma\subset H$ for the diagonal image of $G(\mathbb Q)$, sending each projective rational unit to its real and finite adelic classes.

### Facts & Assumptions

**Given:** The rational Hamilton algebra and a full order $O$ in it, meaning a subring with identity and a $\mathbb Z$-basis that is a $\mathbb Q$-basis of $B$.

[F1] The Hamilton product is bilinear in the four coordinates. The norm is $N(x)=\sum_{r=0}^3x_r^2$, with $x\bar x=N(x)1$, where $\bar x$ negates the three imaginary coordinates. The inverse is $\bar x/N(x)$ when $N(x)\ne0$. Over a field, a quaternion is a unit exactly when its norm is nonzero. Each local algebra $B_\ell$ has the topology of four coordinates over the metric field $\mathbb Q_\ell$. Its units carry the subspace topology and have continuous multiplication and inversion ([def-author-6f52147f3ab3-hamilton-order-completions](item-def-author-6f52147f3ab3-hamilton-order-completions)).

[F2] The projective group is a finite-type group scheme over $\mathbb Q$ with an affine model given by polynomial equations. For a field extension $F/\mathbb Q$, its field points are the units of $B_F$ modulo $F^\times$. The affine model's points over $\mathbb A_f$ are identified as a group with $\widehat B^\times/\mathbb A_f^\times$ and hence with the restricted product for the given order. For a unit $x$ the comparison uses matrix entries $t_{rs}=x_rx_s/N(x)$ ([def-finite-quaternion-adeles-and-projective-units](item-def-finite-quaternion-adeles-and-projective-units)). Its finite adelic group is the restricted product of $G(\mathbb Q_\ell)=B_\ell^\times/\mathbb Q_\ell^\times$ with compact open subgroup $K_f=\prod_\ell K_\ell$, where $K_\ell$ is the image of $O_\ell^\times$. A restricted product consists of tuples in $K_\ell$ at all but finitely many primes, and its basic opens use $K_\ell$ at all but finitely many primes. The restricted product is a topological group. The rational to finite adelic map is injective ([def-finite-quaternion-adeles-and-projective-units](item-def-finite-quaternion-adeles-and-projective-units)). The local unit subgroup $O_\ell^\times$ is open in the local quaternion units ([def-author-6f52147f3ab3-finite-quaternion-ideles](item-def-author-6f52147f3ab3-finite-quaternion-ideles)).

[F3] A subset of a group is a subgroup if it contains the ambient identity and is closed under multiplication and inverses ([math:def-subgroup](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-subgroup)). If $Z$ is a subgroup, the left cosets $uZ$ and $vZ$ are equal exactly when $v^{-1}u\in Z$ ([math:lem-coset-membership-and-equality](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-coset-membership-and-equality)). A subgroup $Z$ of a group $U$ is normal when $gZg^{-1}=Z$ for every $g\in U$ ([math:def-normal-subgroup](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-normal-subgroup)). The cosets of a normal subgroup form a group whose product and inverse are induced by those of representatives ([math:def-quotient-group](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-quotient-group), [math:thm-quotient-group-laws](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-quotient-group-laws)). The quotient topology tests openness by inverse image ([math:def-quotient-topology](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-quotient-topology)). The product topology has basic opens that restrict finitely many coordinates ([math:def-product-topology](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-product-topology)). A topological group has continuous multiplication and inversion ([math:def-topological-group](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-topological-group)).

[F4] Hausdorff means distinct points have disjoint open neighborhoods. Metrizable spaces are Hausdorff, and singletons in Hausdorff spaces are closed ([math:def-hausdorff-space](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-hausdorff-space)). A space is locally compact if each point has a compact neighborhood ([math:def-locally-compact-space](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-locally-compact-space)). A subset is compact when every open cover in its subspace topology has a finite subcover ([math:def-compact-space](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-compact-space)). For $n\ge1$, a closed, Euclidean-bounded subset of the product of $n$ real lines is compact in its subspace topology ([math:cor-heine-borel-in-the-product-topology](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/cor-heine-borel-in-the-product-topology), claim 2). Continuous images of compact spaces are compact ([math:thm-compactness-under-continuous-maps](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-compactness-under-continuous-maps)), and finite products of compact spaces are compact ([math:thm-finite-products-of-compact-spaces](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-finite-products-of-compact-spaces)).

[F5] The Euclidean metric on $\mathbb R^4$ is $d_2(x,y)=\sqrt{\sum_{r=0}^3(x_r-y_r)^2}$ ([math:lem-metrics-on-rn](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-metrics-on-rn)), and its topology is the product of the four real-line topologies ([math:lem-product-topology-on-rn](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-product-topology-on-rn)). Subspace opens are traces of ambient opens, and the subspace inclusion is continuous ([math:def-subspace-topology-top](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-subspace-topology-top)). Finite sums and products of continuous real functions are continuous, and a quotient is continuous where the denominator is nonzero ([math:lem-algebra-of-continuous-real-maps-on-a-space](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-algebra-of-continuous-real-maps-on-a-space)).

[F6] The Cauchy-sequence real field is complete in the sense of the least-upper-bound property ([math:cor-cauchy-reals-lub-complete](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/cor-cauchy-reals-lub-complete)). In an ordered field with this property each nonnegative element has a nonnegative square root ([math:thm-of-square-roots](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-of-square-roots)). In a complete ordered field every nonzero square is positive ([math:thm-of-square-roots](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-of-square-roots)). Positive elements of an ordered field are closed under addition ([math:def-ordered-field](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-ordered-field)).

[F7] The canonical inclusion of $\mathbb Q$ in $\mathbb R$ is a field embedding ([math:lem-rat-embeds-dense](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-rat-embeds-dense)). The diagonal finite embedding makes $\mathbb A_f$ a commutative unital $\mathbb Q$-algebra ([def-author-6f52147f3ab3-finite-scalar-adeles-and-ideles](item-def-author-6f52147f3ab3-finite-scalar-adeles-and-ideles)). The product of commutative unital rings is a commutative unital ring with componentwise operations and identity ([math:def-product-ring](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-product-ring)). A unital ring homomorphism from $\mathbb Q$ to such a ring gives a $\mathbb Q$-algebra, and maps between such algebras commute with their maps from $\mathbb Q$ ([math:def-algebra-over-a-commutative-ring](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-algebra-over-a-commutative-ring)). A map of rings is a unital ring homomorphism exactly when it preserves addition, multiplication and the multiplicative identity ([math:def-ring-homomorphism](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-ring-homomorphism)). A unital ring homomorphism $f$ sends any unit $u$ to a unit with inverse $f(u^{-1})$ ([math:lem-ring-homomorphism-basic-properties](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-ring-homomorphism-basic-properties), clause 3).

[F8] An affine scheme is isomorphic to the spectrum of its coordinate ring ([math:def-affine-scheme](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-affine-scheme)). A morphism over $\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb Q$ commutes with the structure maps to that base ([math:def-scheme-over-base](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-scheme-over-base)). For an affine $\mathbb Q$-scheme with coordinate ring $C$ and a commutative unital $\mathbb Q$-algebra $R$, an $R$-point is a morphism $\operatorname{Spec}R\to\operatorname{Spec}C$ over $\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb Q$ ([math:def-group-scheme-over-a-field](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-group-scheme-over-a-field)). Under the contravariant affine correspondence these are the unital ring maps $C\to R$ that commute with the coefficient maps from $\mathbb Q$ ([math:thm-affine-scheme-ring-anti-equivalence](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-affine-scheme-ring-anti-equivalence), [math:def-algebra-over-a-commutative-ring](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-algebra-over-a-commutative-ring)). For a finite-type group scheme over $\mathbb Q$, maps of commutative unital $\mathbb Q$-algebras induce group homomorphisms on points in the same direction ([math:def-group-scheme-over-a-field](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-group-scheme-over-a-field)).

[F9] A map between topological spaces is continuous exactly when the inverse image of every open set is open (condition (b)). It suffices to test the inverse images of the members of a basis for the target topology (condition (d) for a basis). A continuous map pulls back closed sets to closed sets (condition (c)) ([math:thm-continuity-characterisations-top](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-continuity-characterisations-top)). Every open set is a union of basic open sets, so each point of an open set lies in a basic open set contained in it ([math:def-topology-basis-subbasis](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-topology-basis-subbasis)). Arbitrary unions and finite intersections of open sets are open, and finite intersections of closed sets are closed ([math:def-topological-space](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-topological-space), axioms (T1)–(T3) and (C1)–(C2)).

[F10] In a topological group every left and right translation is a homeomorphism ([math:lem-topological-group-translations-and-inversion](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-topological-group-translations-and-inversion)). A map is open when it sends every open set to an open set. A homeomorphism is open because its inverse is continuous ([math:def-homeomorphism-and-open-maps](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-homeomorphism-and-open-maps)).

[F11] The open metric ball about $x_0$ of radius $r>0$ consists of the points $x$ with $d(x_0,x)<r$ ([math:def-metric-ball](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-metric-ball)). A subset of a metric space is bounded if it is empty or is contained in an open ball ([math:def-metric-bounded-diameter](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-metric-bounded-diameter)).

[F12] The direct product of two groups has componentwise multiplication and inversion, and its identity is the pair of identities ([math:def-external-direct-product-of-groups](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-external-direct-product-of-groups), [math:thm-external-direct-product-is-a-group](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-external-direct-product-is-a-group)). A map of groups that preserves multiplication is a group homomorphism ([math:def-group-homomorphism](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-group-homomorphism)). A bijective group homomorphism is a group isomorphism ([math:def-group-isomorphism-and-automorphism](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-group-isomorphism-and-automorphism)).

### Justification

**Given:** The rational Hamilton algebra, a full order and the topologies specified above.

**Goal:** Verify the real and full adelic group constructions and show that $G_\infty$ is compact Hausdorff, $H$ is locally compact Hausdorff and the rational map into $H$ is injective.

1.1 The real coordinate projections are continuous by the Euclidean product description. The Hamilton product has polynomial coordinates, and inversion has quotient coordinates with nonzero denominator on the units. On the real unit domains these formulas are continuous coordinatewise by [F5], using the product and subspace topologies for the domain projections. The preimages of basic open boxes under these coordinate formulas are finite intersections of open coordinate preimages, so the maps into the ambient space are continuous by the basis test in [F9]. Both operations take values in the units, so the preimage of a subspace open set is the preimage of its ambient open set by the trace description in [F5]. This gives continuity into the unit group by the open-set test in [F9]. For the local unit groups, continuity holds by [F1]. [F1, F3, F5, F9]

1.2 The diagonal rational coefficient map preserves addition, multiplication and the identity in each component by [F7]. Its image is central because the product ring is commutative. The projections are unital ring maps that respect these rational coefficient maps. Write $C$ for the coordinate ring of the affine matrix model of $G$ in [F2]. Write $\eta_C:\mathbb Q\to C$ and $\eta_R:\mathbb Q\to R$ for the coefficient maps. For a ring map $u:C\to R$, the condition $u\circ\eta_C=\eta_R$ is equivalent by contravariance and faithfulness in [F8] to $\operatorname{Spec}(\eta_C)\circ\operatorname{Spec}(u)=\operatorname{Spec}(\eta_R)$. This is the condition that the scheme morphism commutes with the structure maps to $\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb Q$. A map $C\to\mathbb A$ of $\mathbb Q$-algebras is exactly a pair of such maps to $\mathbb R$ and $\mathbb A_f$, since pairing the maps preserves operations, identity and rational scalars componentwise. This yields the asserted bijection on points by [F8]. By [F2] the real field points give $G_\infty$ and the finite scheme points give the projective idele group with the restricted-product description, namely $G_f$. By naturality in [F8], both projections induce group homomorphisms on points. The resulting map to the direct product preserves multiplication in each component, hence is a group homomorphism for the componentwise law in [F12]. Since it is bijective, [F12] makes it an isomorphism. [F2, F7, F8, F12]

2.1 For a real unit $x$, at least one coordinate is nonzero by [F1]. By [F6] each nonzero coordinate has a positive square. Dropping the zero squares leaves a nonempty sum of positive terms, and repeated addition preserves positivity. Thus $N(x)>0$. By [F6] there is $r\ge 0$ with $r^2=N(x)$. Then $r>0$ and $N(r^{-1}x)=r^{-2}N(x)=1$, so every real projective class has a representative on the sphere $S=\{x\in\mathbb R^4:N(x)=1\}$. The coordinate projections and finite sums and products in [F5] give continuity of $N$ on $\mathbb R^4$. Since the real line is metrizable, $\{1\}$ is closed by [F4], and $S=N^{-1}(\{1\})$ is closed by [F9]. Each point of $S$ has $d_2(0,x)=\sqrt{N(x)}=1<2$, so $S$ lies in the open Euclidean ball of radius $2$ about $0$ and is bounded by [F11]. The topological form of Heine–Borel in [F4] gives compactness in the Euclidean subspace topology, which is inherited from the product topology by [F5]. For an open set $W$ of $G_\infty$, its preimage under the scalar quotient map is open in $B_{\mathbb R}^\times$ by [F3]. Intersecting this preimage with $S$ gives an open set of $S$ by the subspace topology in [F5]. Thus the restricted quotient map from $S$ is continuous by [F9]. Its image is $G_\infty$, which is therefore compact by [F4]. [F1, F3, F4, F5, F6, F9, F11, step 1.1]

2.2 In each real or local unit group, the nonzero scalars form a subgroup by [F3] because they contain the ambient identity and are closed under products and inverses. For any quaternion unit $g$ and nonzero scalar $s$, $g(s1)g^{-1}=s1$ because $s1$ commutes with $g$. Thus conjugation fixes the scalar subgroup pointwise and it is normal by [F3], so the coset laws apply. The scalars are precisely the intersection, within the units, of the three inverse images of $\{0\}$ under the imaginary coordinate projections. These projections are continuous by the coordinate product topology and subspace topology. The coefficient fields $\mathbb R$ and $\mathbb Q_\ell$ are metrizable, so $\{0\}$ is closed by [F4]. The three inverse images are closed by [F9], so their finite intersection is closed by the closed-set axiom there. Write $Z$ for the scalar subgroup and $p$ for the scalar quotient map. By [F3], $p(u)=p(v)$ exactly when $v^{-1}u\in Z$. For an open set $V$, this gives $p^{-1}(p(V))=\bigcup_{v\in V}vZ=\bigcup_{z\in Z}Vz$. The translates are open by [F10], applied to the unit groups in step 1.1, so their union is open by [F9] and $p$ is open by the quotient topology. For open subsets $U,V$ of the unit group, $(p\times p)(U\times V)=p(U)\times p(V)$ is open by the product topology in [F3]. Every open set in the domain is a union of such rectangles by [F9], and its image is the union of the open images of those rectangles since images commute with unions. Thus $p\times p$ is open by [F9] and [F10], and it is surjective because $p$ is surjective. The pullback under $p\times p$ of a multiplication preimage downstairs is the multiplication preimage upstairs of the corresponding pullback under $p$. The latter is open for an open set downstairs, and its image under $p\times p$ is the multiplication preimage downstairs. The same calculation with $p$ proves that the inversion preimage downstairs is open. Both quotient operations are therefore continuous by the open-set test in [F9]. For distinct scalar cosets of $a$ and $b$, the element $b^{-1}a$ is outside the closed scalar subgroup by [F3]. The map $(v,u)\mapsto(v^{-1},u)$ is continuous because the inverse image of a basic open rectangle $V\times U$ is the open rectangle $\{v:v^{-1}\in V\}\times U$ by continuity of inversion and the product topology in [F3]. The basis test in [F9] therefore applies. Composing with continuous multiplication gives the continuous map $(v,u)\mapsto v^{-1}u$, since inverse images under the composition are successive inverse images of opens. Its preimage of the complement of the scalar subgroup is open and contains $(b,a)$. By [F3] and [F9] it contains an open rectangle about $(b,a)$. The images of the two factors of this rectangle under $p$ are open and disjoint, since equal images of $v,u$ would make $v^{-1}u$ scalar by [F3]. Each $K_\ell$ is open because it is the image of the open local unit subgroup under the open quotient map. [F1, F2, F3, F4, F5, F9, F10, step 1.1]

3.1 For an open set $W\subseteq G(\mathbb Q_\ell)$, every tuple in its preimage under the $\ell$-th coordinate map from $G_f$ has a basic box about it. Intersect the factor at $\ell$ with $W$ and leave the other factors as they are. This is still a restricted-product basic box by [F2]. The preimage is a union of these boxes and is open by [F9], so the coordinate map is continuous by the open-set test there. Distinct tuples can therefore be separated in a coordinate where they differ, using the local Hausdorff property of step 2.2. The real and finite factors are Hausdorff topological groups by step 2.2 and [F2]. The product topology separates points in any differing factor. For the componentwise multiplication and inversion of [F12], a basic open rectangle pulls back to the intersection of the inverse images of its two open factors. Each is open by continuity of the factor operations and the product projection formulas in [F3]. Their intersection is open by [F9]. The basis test in [F9] gives continuity of the two operations on the full product. The subgroup $K_f$ is compact and open in $G_f$ by [F2]. The subspace topology on $G_\infty\times K_f$ is the product of the indicated factor topologies, since basic rectangles restrict to rectangles with open factors. Thus $G_\infty\times K_f$ is compact by [F4] and step 2.1, and is open in $H$. Its left translate by any $h\in H$ is compact and open, by the translation homeomorphism in [F10] and the compact-image assertion in [F4]. The original product contains the identity, so the translate is an open set containing $h$ and is thus a neighborhood of $h$. This proves local compactness by [F4]. [F2, F3, F4, F5, F9, F10, F12, step 2.1, step 2.2]

4.1 The rational coefficient map induces a group homomorphism $G(\mathbb Q)\to H$. Each projection sends the class of a unit of $B$ to its class after extending coefficients in that factor, since the Hamilton coordinates and scalar equivalence are preserved by the embeddings. For $x\in B^\times$, the rational norm $N(x)$ is nonzero by [F1], so it is a unit of $\mathbb Q$. By [F7], $\iota_f(N(x))$ is a unit with inverse $\iota_f(N(x)^{-1})$. The formula $t_{rs}=x_rx_s/N(x)$ in [F2] therefore commutes with the finite coefficient map, which preserves sums, products and this inverse. Under the finite comparison in [F2], this component is therefore the diagonal projective idele map. The rational homomorphism into $H$ is injective because that finite component is injective. [F1, F2, F7, F8, step 1.2] ∎


## Ramanujan–Petersson for weight-two Euler roots

[Open item](item-thm-weight-two-newform-euler-root-bound) · level 5


### Statement

Assume countable choice, meaning a sequence of nonempty sets has a choice function. Let $f$ be a normalized primitive holomorphic cusp form of weight two, level $N$ and Dirichlet character $\epsilon$. This means that $f$ is new at level $N$, is a simultaneous eigenfunction of the classical Hecke operators and has first Fourier coefficient one. For a prime $\ell\nmid N$ let $a_\ell$ be its classical Hecke eigenvalue at $\ell$. Then each root of the Hecke polynomial
$$P_\ell(X)=X^2-a_\ell X+\ell\epsilon(\ell)$$
has complex absolute value $\sqrt\ell$, the positive square root of $\ell$.

Write $L=\mathrm{GL}_2(\mathbb A)$ for the general linear group over the rational adeles, with its restricted-product topology, and $Z$ for its scalar subgroup. The continuous adelization $\Phi_f:L\to\mathbb C$ is uniquely determined by left invariance under the diagonally embedded $\mathrm{GL}_2(\mathbb Q)$, the finite right covariance $\Phi_f(gk)=\epsilon(d(k))^{-1}\Phi_f(g)$ for $g\in L$ and $k\in K_0(N)$, and the real restriction $\Phi_f(h_\infty)=(f|_2h)(i)$ for positive-determinant real $h$, inserted at the real place with identity at every finite place. Here $K_0(N)$ is the subgroup of finite tuples $k=(k_p)_p$ in $\prod_p\mathrm{GL}_2(\mathbb Z_p)$ with lower left entry in $N\mathbb Z_p$ at every $p$, embedded with real component the identity. For each maximal prime power $p^{e_p}$ dividing $N$, reduce the lower right entry of $k_p$ modulo $p^{e_p}$. The Chinese remainder map combines these residues into the unit class $d(k)$ modulo $N$. At $N=1$ this is the sole unit class.

The closed span of right translates of $\Phi_f$ in the cuspidal $L^2$ space with its unitary central character gives the irreducible unitary adelic representation of $f$. In this model the squared norm integrates the absolute square on $Z\mathrm{GL}_2(\mathbb Q)\backslash L$ with its quotient topology and a nonzero right-$L$-invariant Radon measure. Let $\pi_\ell$ be the factor at $G_\ell=\mathrm{GL}_2(\mathbb Q_\ell)$, where $\mathbb Q_\ell$ is the $\ell$-adic field. It is unramified, meaning it has a nonzero vector fixed by the compact open subgroup $K=\mathrm{GL}_2(\mathbb Z_\ell)$, where $\mathbb Z_\ell$ is the local integer ring. The two roots of $P_\ell$, divided by $\sqrt\ell$, are its unit-normalized Satake parameters. These are the unordered pair of nonzero complex numbers, counted with multiplicity, giving the scalar of each spherical Hecke operator on the $K$-fixed line by evaluation of its normalized Satake transform. In particular each parameter has absolute value one.

### Statement references

[Classical normalized newform](item-def-author-e2fc0f4318ab-normalized-weight-two-newform)

[Weight-two adelic conventions](item-lem-author-e2fc0f4318ab-weight-two-adelic-hecke-scalars)

[Normalized spherical character and Satake parameters](item-thm-normalized-degree-two-spherical-character)

[Adelic matrix group and its topology](item-def-author-34e2e6a0519f-split-adelic-matrix-groups)

[General linear groups of degree two](item-def-degree-two-projective-linear-group)

[Local field, integral subgroup and topology](item-lem-author-d45da46285be-triangular-cosets)

[Normalized Satake transform](item-def-author-d45da46285be-satake-transform)

[Spherical Hecke operators](item-def-author-d45da46285be-spherical-hecke-algebra)

[Quotient topology](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-quotient-topology)

[Radon measure](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-radon-measure-on-an-lch-space)

[Prime powers of the level](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-fundamental-theorem-of-arithmetic)

[Local integer rings and their residue classes](item-def-author-24fda87362bc-local-adic-valuation)

[Chinese remainder isomorphism on units](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-unit-group-chinese-remainder-decomposition)

### Facts & Assumptions

[F1] The Fourier coefficient indexed by $\ell$ equals the classical $T_\ell$ eigenvalue, since $f$ has first Fourier coefficient one and $\ell\nmid N$ ([def-author-e2fc0f4318ab-weight-two-cusp-form](item-def-author-e2fc0f4318ab-weight-two-cusp-form), [def-author-e2fc0f4318ab-normalized-weight-two-newform](item-def-author-e2fc0f4318ab-normalized-weight-two-newform), [lem-author-e2fc0f4318ab-classical-hecke-first-coefficient](item-lem-author-e2fc0f4318ab-classical-hecke-first-coefficient)).

[F2] Each of the two spherical Hecke operators is a finite sum of translates, one per contributing coset $xK$. Evaluation of the normalized Satake transform at the pair $(u,v)$ gives the scalar on the fixed line. For the two operators $\mathcal T_\ell$ and $\mathcal R_\ell$ of the trace and product calculation these scalars are $\sqrt\ell(u+v)$ and $uv$ on an irreducible admissible smooth local representation with a nonzero $K$-fixed space and unitary realization. In the spherical-character theorem, a vector is smooth if it is fixed by an open subgroup. On the weight-two adelization, once these operators act by scalars, those scalars are $a_\ell$ and $\epsilon(\ell)$, respectively ([def-author-d45da46285be-spherical-hecke-algebra](item-def-author-d45da46285be-spherical-hecke-algebra), [def-author-d45da46285be-satake-transform](item-def-author-d45da46285be-satake-transform), [thm-normalized-degree-two-spherical-character](item-thm-normalized-degree-two-spherical-character), [lem-author-e2fc0f4318ab-spherical-trace-and-product](item-lem-author-e2fc0f4318ab-spherical-trace-and-product), [lem-author-e2fc0f4318ab-weight-two-adelic-hecke-scalars](item-lem-author-e2fc0f4318ab-weight-two-adelic-hecke-scalars)).

[F3] Complex absolute value is multiplicative and agrees with the usual positive real value on positive reals. Complex absolute value also satisfies the triangle inequality and is unchanged by negation. The positive square root of $\ell$ exists and is nonzero since the real numbers form a complete ordered field and $\ell>0$ ([math:def-complex-conjugate-real-imaginary-part-and-modulus](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-complex-conjugate-real-imaginary-part-and-modulus), [math:lem-complex-conjugation-and-modulus-laws](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-complex-conjugation-and-modulus-laws), [math:thm-of-square-roots](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-of-square-roots), [math:cor-cauchy-reals-lub-complete](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/cor-cauchy-reals-lub-complete), [math:def-prime](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-prime)).

[F4] Use the adelization and representation results below for strong approximation, the continuous lift, the cuspidal Hilbert space, the quotient Haar measure, the newform correspondence, the restricted tensor product and the local smooth representation.

[F5] Deligne’s theorem gives the absolute values of both roots in the stated classical normalization.

[F6] The local matrix group and integral subgroup use the entry topologies, and $K$ is compact and open. For every finite prime $p$, the ring $\mathbb Z_p$ is open in $\mathbb Q_p$. The adelic group uses coordinatewise multiplication and the restricted-product topology. Each place embeds by inserting the identity at every other place ([def-degree-two-projective-linear-group](item-def-degree-two-projective-linear-group), [lem-author-d45da46285be-triangular-cosets](item-lem-author-d45da46285be-triangular-cosets), [def-author-34e2e6a0519f-split-adelic-matrix-groups](item-def-author-34e2e6a0519f-split-adelic-matrix-groups)). The adelic and local matrix groups are topological groups, so left and right translations by fixed elements are homeomorphisms ([def-author-34e2e6a0519f-split-adelic-matrix-groups](item-def-author-34e2e6a0519f-split-adelic-matrix-groups)).

[F7] In the quotient topology a set is open if its preimage is open. A nonzero Radon measure is inner regular on open sets, so the whole space contains a compact subset of positive measure ([math:def-quotient-topology](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-quotient-topology), [math:def-radon-measure-on-an-lch-space](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-radon-measure-on-an-lch-space)). Measures are finitely subadditive. A nonnegative measurable function of zero integral vanishes almost everywhere ([math:thm-finite-and-countable-subadditivity-of-measures](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-finite-and-countable-subadditivity-of-measures), [math:thm-nonnegative-integral-zero-iff-zero-almost-everywhere](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-nonnegative-integral-zero-iff-zero-almost-everywhere)). The open sets generate the Borel sigma-algebra of a topological space. A map is measurable if the preimage of each set in a generating family for the target sigma-algebra is measurable. [math:def-borel-sigma-algebra](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-borel-sigma-algebra) [math:thm-generating-family-criterion-for-measurable-functions](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-generating-family-criterion-for-measurable-functions) The open rays of a linear order generate its order topology ([math:def-order-topology-on-a-linearly-ordered-set](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-order-topology-on-a-linearly-ordered-set)). A cover of a compact subset by ambient open sets has a finite subcover. [math:lem-compactness-of-a-subspace-is-ambient](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-compactness-of-a-subspace-is-ambient) A compact subset of a Hausdorff space is closed and hence Borel. [math:thm-compact-subset-of-a-hausdorff-space-is-closed](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-compact-subset-of-a-hausdorff-space-is-closed) [math:def-borel-sigma-algebra](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-borel-sigma-algebra) A measure is monotone under inclusion of measurable sets. [math:prop-measure-monotonicity](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/prop-measure-monotonicity)

[F8] The Hilbert tensor product completes the span of elementary tensors with the product pairing. Under countable choice a bounded linear map on a dense linear subspace extends to the completion when the target is complete. For the action of a group on the first tensor factor, the fixed space is the tensor product of the first factor's fixed space with the second factor. A scalar action by a finite linear combination of group operators on the local fixed space gives the same scalar on the tensor fixed space ([math:lem-nrqm-composites-hilbert-tensor-construction](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-nrqm-composites-hilbert-tensor-construction), [math:thm-extension-of-a-bounded-map-from-a-dense-subspace](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-extension-of-a-bounded-map-from-a-dense-subspace), [lem-author-c3a9b9243c74-fixed-space-of-tensor-factor](item-lem-author-c3a9b9243c74-fixed-space-of-tensor-factor)).

[F9] The group $K_0(N)$ is defined by the lower left entry condition at the finite places. The maps $a(k),d(k)$ take the upper left and lower right unit classes modulo $N$ by combining local reductions at the maximal prime powers of $N$. Prime factorization makes these moduli pairwise coprime, and the Chinese remainder isomorphism on unit groups applies, including the empty list for $N=1$. These classes multiply under products, since the reductions are upper triangular. The finite part of a rational matrix in $\Gamma_0(N)$ gives its ordinary residue classes. The weight-two slash action composes in the order of matrix multiplication. Dirichlet character values on units have modulus one, and $\epsilon(-1)=1$ by the weight-two transformation at $-I$. [lem-author-e2fc0f4318ab-weight-two-adelic-hecke-scalars](item-lem-author-e2fc0f4318ab-weight-two-adelic-hecke-scalars) [def-author-e2fc0f4318ab-weight-two-cusp-form](item-def-author-e2fc0f4318ab-weight-two-cusp-form) [math:def-dirichlet-character-modulo-q](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-dirichlet-character-modulo-q) [math:lem-dirichlet-character-values](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-dirichlet-character-values) [def-author-24fda87362bc-local-adic-valuation](item-def-author-24fda87362bc-local-adic-valuation) [math:thm-fundamental-theorem-of-arithmetic](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-fundamental-theorem-of-arithmetic) [math:thm-unit-group-chinese-remainder-decomposition](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-unit-group-chinese-remainder-decomposition)

[F10] An idele is a tuple of nonzero local scalars that are units at all but finitely many finite places. The valuation $v_p$ is additive under multiplication, with $v_p(p)=1$ and $v_p(q)=0$ for any other prime $q$. On a nonzero rational $m/n$ it is the difference of the integer valuations of $m$ and $n$. Determinant is multiplicative, and the determinant of an adelic invertible matrix is an idele. [def-author-6f52147f3ab3-finite-scalar-adeles-and-ideles](item-def-author-6f52147f3ab3-finite-scalar-adeles-and-ideles) [lem-author-d45da46285be-triangular-cosets](item-lem-author-d45da46285be-triangular-cosets) [math:thm-determinant-multiplicative](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-determinant-multiplicative) [def-author-24fda87362bc-local-adic-valuation](item-def-author-24fda87362bc-local-adic-valuation) [math:lem-p-adic-valuation-on-q](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-p-adic-valuation-on-q) The absolute value on the completion $\mathbb Q_p$ is nonarchimedean ([math:thm-p-adic-completion-is-a-field](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-p-adic-completion-is-a-field)). Under countable choice $\mathbb Q_p$ is locally compact in its $p$-adic topology. [math:cor-p-adic-field-is-locally-compact-and-totally-disconnected](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/cor-p-adic-field-is-locally-compact-and-totally-disconnected) For a real $r$ with $|r|<1$, the powers $r^n$ converge to zero. [math:lem-geometric-sequence-null](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-geometric-sequence-null)

[F11] The entrywise formula $ad-bc$ for the determinant is continuous on each local matrix group because field addition, negation and multiplication are continuous. The entry-topology argument is in step 1.2 of the split group construction [def-author-34e2e6a0519f-split-adelic-matrix-groups](item-def-author-34e2e6a0519f-split-adelic-matrix-groups). A unitary representation is a homomorphism into the bijective complex-linear isometries of a complex Hilbert space with norm-continuous vector orbits [math:def-strongly-continuous-unitary-representation](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-strongly-continuous-unitary-representation).

[F12] The distinct left cosets of a subgroup partition its ambient group. [math:lem-coset-partition](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-coset-partition) An arbitrary union of open sets is open. [math:def-topological-space](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-topological-space) A closed subspace of a compact space is compact. [math:thm-closed-subspace-of-a-compact-space-is-compact](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-closed-subspace-of-a-compact-space-is-compact)

### External input

Strong approximation expresses each element of $L$ as $q h_\infty k$ with $q\in\mathrm{GL}_2(\mathbb Q)$, $h\in\mathrm{GL}_2^+(\mathbb R)$ and $k\in K_0(N)$. [Eigenspaces of Newforms with Nontrivial Character](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2307.07804#page=12)

Trotabas evaluates his classical nebentypus $\chi$ on the upper left entry of a matrix in $\Gamma_0(N)$. His Definition 5.1 gives a left rational invariant lift with value $\det(h)^{k/2}(ci+d)^{-k}f(hi)$ at $h_\infty$ in weight $k$, and finite right covariance through $d(k)$. It is continuous and transforms under the center by a unitary character. The cuspidal $L^2$ model uses the squared norm in the statement and realizes the action by right translation. An irreducible unitary representation of $L$ is a restricted Hilbert tensor product of irreducible local unitary representations. It is built by enlarging finite tensor products with the specified unit reference vectors. An element inserted at one place acts on the factor there. [Modular Forms and Automorphic Representations](https://math.stanford.edu/~conrad/conversesem/refs/trotabas.pdf#page=15)

The $L^2$ integral uses the quotient of Haar measure on $Z\mathrm{GL}_2(\mathbb Q)\backslash L$, a nonzero right-invariant Radon measure. Its scale is immaterial to the norm-zero condition. [Average Values of Modular L-Series via the Relative Trace Formula](https://arxiv.org/pdf/math/0510113#page=24)

The adelic lift of a classical holomorphic newform lies in an irreducible unitary cuspidal representation. It has the real restriction in the statement and is fixed by Corbett and Saha’s $K_1(N)$, the subgroup of $K_0(N)$ with upper left entry congruent to one at each prime power in the level. [On the order of vanishing of newforms at cusps](https://webspace.maths.qmul.ac.uk/abhishek.saha/research/ramification-cusps.pdf#page=17)

For a primitive normalized cusp form and a prime away from its level, Deligne's root theorem applies to the polynomial with linear coefficient given by the Fourier coefficient with a minus sign and constant coefficient $\epsilon(\ell)\ell^{k-1}$ in weight $k\ge2$. [La conjecture de Weil : I](https://www.numdam.org/item/PMIHES_1974__43__273_0.pdf#page=31)

For a topologically irreducible unitary Hilbert-space representation of $\mathrm{GL}_n(F)$ over a nonarchimedean local field $F$, the vectors fixed by some compact open subgroup form an invariant subspace with an algebraically irreducible admissible action. Admissibility means that the fixed space for each compact open subgroup is finite dimensional. [Automorphic Representations and L-functions for GL(n)](https://www.math.columbia.edu/~goldfeld/LanglandsBookChapter.pdf#page=3)

### Proof

**Given:** The normalized weight-two newform $f$, its character and level, and the prime $\ell\nmid N$.

**Goal:** Identify the two rescaled roots with the two Satake parameters and determine their absolute values.

1.1 Let $X=Z\mathrm{GL}_2(\mathbb Q)\backslash L$ with the quotient measure $\mu$ of [F4]. By [F6], right translation $R_x$ on $L$ is a homeomorphism. It descends to a map $\bar R_x$ on left cosets. For the quotient map $q:L\to X$ and any open $U\subset X$, the identity $q^{-1}(\bar R_x^{-1}(U))=R_x^{-1}(q^{-1}(U))$ gives continuity of $\bar R_x$ by [F7]. The same argument for $x^{-1}$ gives continuity of the inverse, so right translation on $X$ is a homeomorphism. Any nonempty open subset of $X$ has positive measure. Indeed its right translates are open and cover $X$ by transitivity. If the subset had measure zero, its translates would be null by right invariance. By [F7], finitely many of those ambient open translates cover the compact positive-measure subset $C$ from [F7]. Their union $B$ is open and hence Borel, and $C$ is Borel since the Radon measure is on a Hausdorff space. Finite subadditivity gives $\mu(B)=0$. As $C\subseteq B$, monotonicity gives $\mu(C)\le\mu(B)=0$, a contradiction. If a continuous function $D$ on $L$ is left rational invariant and has the unitary central covariance of $\Phi_f$, then $|D|^2$ is constant on the quotient fibers because the central character has modulus one. The triangle inequality and invariance under negation in [F3] give $\bigl||z|-|w|\bigr|\le |z-w|$ by writing $z=w+(z-w)$ and $w=z+(w-z)$. Factoring the difference of squares gives $\bigl||z|^2-|w|^2\bigr|\le (|z|+|w|)|z-w|$. For $|z-w|<1$ the factor $|z|+|w|$ is bounded by $2|w|+1$, proving continuity at each $w$. Thus $|D|^2$ is continuous and descends to a continuous function on $X$ with values in the usual real half-line by the quotient topology. Put $Y=[0,+\infty]$ with the order topology of [F7]. For a finite cut $a$, the two open rays in $Y$ restrict to $[0,a)$ and $(a,\infty)$ in the finite half-line. At the infinite cut the restrictions are the whole finite half-line and the empty set. These restrictions are relatively open and the rays generate the topology of $Y$. Every open set of $Y$ meets $[0,\infty)$ in a relatively open set, so the descended absolute square has open preimages for open sets of $Y$. These preimages belong to $\mathcal B(X)$, the sigma-algebra of the Radon measure. The open sets of $Y$ generate $\mathcal B(Y)$, so the generating-family criterion of [F7] applies to the descended absolute square as a map $(X,\mathcal B(X))\to(Y,\mathcal B(Y))$. If $D$ represents the zero $L^2$ class, the descended absolute square has integral zero. By [F7] it vanishes almost everywhere. Its positive set is open and null, so by full support it is empty. Thus $D$ vanishes everywhere. [F3, F4, F6, F7]

1.2 Since $a_1=1$, take $z=x+iy$ in the upper half-plane with $f(z)\ne0$. The real matrix $h=\begin{pmatrix}y&x\\0&1\end{pmatrix}$ has positive determinant, sends $i$ to $z$ and gives $(f|_2h)(i)\ne0$. For Trotabas's lift in [F4], take the Dirichlet character $\chi$ defined on units by $\chi(u)=\epsilon(u)^{-1}$ and extended by zero on nonunits. For every $\gamma\in\Gamma_0(N)$ with diagonal entries $a_\gamma,d_\gamma$, the determinant gives $a_\gamma d_\gamma\equiv 1\pmod N$. Thus $\chi(a_\gamma)=\epsilon(d_\gamma)$, so $f$ satisfies Trotabas's classical transformation law. For any unit class $d_0$ and integer representatives of it and its inverse $a_0$, the matrix $\gamma=\begin{pmatrix}a_0&(a_0d_0-1)/N\\N&d_0\end{pmatrix}$ belongs to $\Gamma_0(N)$. Its finite part $\gamma_f$ has lower right class $d_0$ and upper left class $a_0$. On this part, a left rational invariant lift with that real restriction satisfies $\Phi(h_\infty\gamma_f)=\Phi((\gamma^{-1}h)_\infty)=\epsilon(d_0)^{-1}(f|_2h)(i)$, by the slash composition and transformation laws. The lower right character of the continuous lift thus equals $\epsilon^{-1}$. Strong approximation makes left rational invariance, the real restriction and this covariance determine the lift uniquely: they give the value $\epsilon(d(k))^{-1}(f|_2 h)(i)$ at every $q h_\infty k$. The continuous lift supplied by Trotabas therefore gives the $\Phi_f$ in the statement. [F1, F4, F9, given]

2.1 Let $\Psi$ be Corbett and Saha’s adelic newform. For $k\in K_0(N)$, split off the finite scalar tuple whose component at each $p\mid N$ is the upper left entry of $k_p$ and is one elsewhere. The entries at $p\mid N$ are units. Dividing out this tuple leaves the lower left entries divisible by their local levels and makes the upper left entries one at $p\mid N$, so the quotient lies in Corbett and Saha’s $K_1(N)$ and fixes $\Psi$ by [F4]. Scalar unit tuples reducing to one at those prime powers belong to $K_1(N)$, so their central character is one. The central character therefore gives right covariance of $\Psi$ by a character $\xi(a(k))$ of the upper left residue class. Using $\gamma_f$ and the nonzero real restriction in step 1.2 gives $\xi(a_0)=\epsilon(d_0)^{-1}=\epsilon(a_0)$ for every unit class $a_0$. [F4, F9, step 1.2]

2.2 By steps 1.1 and 1.2 the class $[\Phi_f]$ is nonzero. Embed $G_\ell$ in $L$ by inserting the identity at every place other than $\ell$. This is a continuous homomorphism because multiplication is coordinatewise and the preimage of a basic restricted-product box is empty or its open $\ell$-component. Since $\ell\nmid N$, the embedded $K$ lies in $K_0(N)$ and has $d(k)=1$ modulo $N$. The finite right covariance fixes $\Phi_f$ under $K$ and therefore fixes its class. [F4, F6, F9, given, step 1.1, step 1.2]

3.1 For an idele $s$, put $r(s)=\prod_{p}p^{v_p(s_p)}\in\mathbb Q_{>0}$. This product has finitely many nontrivial factors, and $v_p(r(s))=v_p(s_p)$ by [F10], so $s_f/r(s)$ has finite unit components. Using the Chinese remainder isomorphism in [F9], define $\eta(s)=\epsilon((s_f/r(s))\bmod N)$ from the reductions of these units. Additivity of the valuations gives $r(st)=r(s)r(t)$, so multiplicativity of reduction and the unit character law make $\eta$ a unitary character. For $p\mid N$ write $e=e_p\ge1$. The projection to the $e$th coordinate of the compatible residue model in [F9] has kernel $p^e\mathbb Z_p$. Indeed, if $x_e=0$ for a compatible tuple $x=(x_n)_n$, then each $x_{n+e}$ is represented by a multiple of $p^e$. Dividing those representatives by $p^e$ gives well-defined classes modulo $p^n$, since changing a representative changes the quotient by a multiple of $p^n$. The resulting classes are compatible because reduction from modulus $p^{n+e+1}$ to $p^{n+e}$ becomes reduction from $p^{n+1}$ to $p^n$ after division. They define $y\in\mathbb Z_p$, and reduction of $x_{n+e}$ modulo $p^n$ gives $x_n$, so $x=p^e y$ in every coordinate. Conversely a multiple of $p^e$ projects to zero. Thus each fiber is a translate of $p^e\mathbb Z_p$. Multiplication by $p^e$ and translation are homeomorphisms of $\mathbb Q_p$ by continuity of field operations and their inverse maps, so the fibers are open by [F6]. At each nonunit finite place of $s$, the open ball of radius $|s_p|_p$ about $s_p$ fixes its valuation. If $|x-s_p|_p<|s_p|_p$, applying [F10] to $x=s_p+(x-s_p)$ gives $|x|_p\le |s_p|_p$. Applying it to $s_p=x+(s_p-x)$ gives $|s_p|_p\le \max(|x|_p,|x-s_p|_p)$, which forces $|x|_p\ge |s_p|_p$. Equality of absolute values gives $v_p(x)=v_p(s_p)$. In particular each unit has a radius-one ball of units, so the local unit group is open. Require units at all other finite places, which fixes $r(s)$. At each $p\mid N$, also restrict the entry divided by this fixed $r(s)$ to its residue fiber. These finitely many extra restrictions are open because scaling by $r(s)^{-1}$ is a homeomorphism. The resulting open box contains $s$ and $\eta$ is constant there. For a nonzero rational $m/n$ with $n>0$, factor $|m|$ and $n$ into primes by [F9]. Their exponents differ by $v_p(m/n)$ by [F10], so $r(m/n)=|m|/n$ for the diagonal idele. The finite quotient by $r(m/n)$ is the sign of $m/n$, and $\epsilon(-1)=1$. Thus $\eta$ is trivial on rational ideles and also on real insertions, where the finite tuple is the identity. The function $\eta(\det g)^{-1}\Psi(g)$ has left rational invariance and the same real restriction as $\Phi_f$. For $k\in K_0(N)$, its determinant has finite unit components and residue $a(k)d(k)$, so step 2.1 gives the twisted function finite right covariance by $\epsilon(d(k))^{-1}$. Strong approximation now identifies the twisted function with $\Phi_f$. Use the same quotient measure for both central characters. The map $M$ of multiplication by $\eta(\det g)^{-1}$ preserves the squared norm and obeys $R(x)M=\eta(\det x)^{-1}MR(x)$ for right translation by $x\in L$. It maps the span of right translates of $\Psi$ isometrically onto that of $\Phi_f$. By [F8], the map and its inverse extend to the two closed spans. Their compositions are identities on the dense spans and hence everywhere. The intertwining identity also holds on the closures by continuity. Therefore the cyclic unitary space of $\Phi_f$ is the determinant twist of the irreducible cyclic space of $\Psi$ and is irreducible, since multiplying the action by nonzero scalars preserves its invariant subspaces. [F4, F6, F8, F9, F10, F11, step 1.2, step 2.1]

4.1 The determinant map $L\to\mathbb A^\times$ is continuous. For a basic idele box, the local determinant preimages of its factors are open by [F11]. Outside the finitely many exceptional places, those factors contain $\mathrm{GL}_2(\mathbb Z_p)$, since an integral invertible matrix and its integral inverse have determinants whose product is $1$. At each point of the preimage, retain the local determinant preimages at the real place and at the finite exceptional places of the target box or the point. Use the integral matrix group at every other place. This gives a basic matrix neighborhood inside the preimage. Thus $\eta\circ\det$ is locally constant by step 3.1. Let $\pi_\Psi$ be the irreducible unitary right-translation action on the cyclic space of $\Psi$ supplied by [F4]. Its orbit maps are norm-continuous. By step 3.1, for every vector $w$ in that space the orbit map of $Mw$ is $x\mapsto\eta(\det x)^{-1}M\pi_\Psi(x)w$. Near any fixed $x_0\in L$ the scalar factor is constant, so this orbit map is norm-continuous there by continuity of $M$ and of the orbit of $w$. The twist acts by bijective complex-linear isometries and satisfies the right-translation group law. Therefore the action on the cyclic space of $\Phi_f$ is strongly continuous and unitary. [F4, F6, F10, F11, step 3.1]

5.1 By steps 3.1 and 4.1, the representation is irreducible and strongly continuous unitary, so the tensor product theorem in [F4] applies. Identify the adelic Hilbert space with $V_\ell\widehat\otimes W$, where $V_\ell$ is the space of $\pi_\ell$ and $W$ is the restricted Hilbert tensor product of the factors at the other places with the same reference vectors. To see the identification, take finite stages containing the exceptional places and $\ell$. Each such stage maps into $V_\ell\widehat\otimes W$ by grouping its elementary tensors into the $\ell$-component and the tensor of the remaining components, which maps into $W$. The product pairing makes this map an isometry on finite linear combinations, and the maps respect the inclusions that insert reference vectors. The union of these stages is dense in the global space because any finite stage can be enlarged to include $\ell$. The images span a dense subspace of $V_\ell\widehat\otimes W$ since the finite-stage tensors are dense in $W$. The isometry and its inverse on the dense image both extend by bounded linear extension. Their compositions equal the identities by continuity on the dense subspaces, so the extended map is onto. The finite-stage action gives $\pi_\ell(x)\otimes I_W$ for $x\in G_\ell$ on the dense span and hence everywhere by continuity. [F4, F8, step 2.2, step 3.1, step 4.1]

6.1 By [F10] and countable choice, $\mathbb Q_\ell$ is a locally compact nonarchimedean valued field in its $\ell$-adic topology. The nonzero elements $\ell^n$ have absolute values $\ell^{-n}\to0$ by the valuation and geometric limit in [F10], so the topology is nondiscrete. Thus $\mathbb Q_\ell$ is a nonarchimedean local field. Apply the local smooth representation result in [F4] with $F=\mathbb Q_\ell$, $n=2$ and the topologically irreducible unitary factor $\pi_\ell$ from step 5.1. Its vectors fixed by a compact open subgroup carry an algebraically irreducible admissible action. To compare with the smoothness convention in [F2], suppose a vector is fixed by an open subgroup $J$ of $G_\ell$. Then $J\cap K$ is open. The left cosets of $J\cap K$ in $K$ partition $K$ by [F12]. Each coset is open in $K$ by restriction of the corresponding translation homeomorphism from [F6]. The complement of $J\cap K$ in $K$ is a union of these open cosets, so it is open by [F12]. Thus $J\cap K$ is closed in the compact space $K$ and is compact by [F12]. The vector is fixed by this compact open subgroup. A vector fixed by a compact open subgroup is fixed by an open subgroup, so the two conventions give the same space. [F2, F4, F6, F10, F12, given, step 5.1]

7.1 The nonzero $K$-fixed class from step 2.2 and the fixed-space identity in [F8] imply $V_\ell^K\ne0$. Since $K$ is compact open, $V_\ell^K$ is also the $K$-fixed subspace of the smooth representation in step 6.1. With countable choice as assumed, the hypotheses of [F2] hold. The operators $\mathcal T_\ell$ and $\mathcal R_\ell$ act on $V_\ell^K$ by $\sqrt\ell(u+v)$ and $uv$. Each is a finite sum of operators from $G_\ell$, so [F8] transfers the same scalars to the tensor fixed space and thus to $[\Phi_f]$. [F2, F6, F8, given, step 2.2, step 5.1, step 6.1]

8.1 For either of the operators $T$ in step 7.1 with scalar $\lambda$, form the function $D=T\Phi_f-\lambda\Phi_f$ using the finite sum of right translates. Each translated function is continuous by composition with a right-translation homeomorphism from [F6]. Right translates preserve left rational invariance and left central covariance because left multiplication commutes with right translation. This sum represents the action on the class $[\Phi_f]$, so $D$ represents the zero class by step 7.1. Step 1.1 gives $T\Phi_f=\lambda\Phi_f$ at every argument. [F2, F4, F6, step 1.1, step 7.1]

9.1 Applying the weight-two comparison in [F2] to these pointwise scalar identities gives $\sqrt\ell(u+v)=a_\ell$ and $uv=\epsilon(\ell)$. Thus $\ell(Y-u)(Y-v)=\ell Y^2-\sqrt\ell a_\ell Y+\ell\epsilon(\ell)=P_\ell(\sqrt\ell Y)$. In particular the root multiset of $P_\ell$ is $\{\sqrt\ell u,\sqrt\ell v\}$, including multiplicities. [F2, F3, step 8.1]

10.1 The form is holomorphic, cuspidal, primitive and normalized, and the prime does not divide the level. By [F1], Deligne’s theorem in [F5] uses the same coefficient $a_\ell$ as in $P_\ell$. Its weight-two conclusion gives absolute value $\sqrt\ell$ for each root. By [F3] and step 9.1 this yields $|u|=|v|=1$, with the identification of the parameters already proved. [F1, F3, F5, given, step 9.1] ∎


## Invariant measure on the projective Hamilton quotient

[Open item](item-lem-author-5b8600b4f4af-hamilton-invariant-quotient) · level 5


### Statement

Assume the Axiom of Choice. For the full adelic projective Hamilton group $H=G(\mathbb A)$ with diagonal rational subgroup $\Gamma=G(\mathbb Q)$, the subgroup $\Gamma$ is closed in $H$ (its complement is open) and discrete (each singleton in $\Gamma$ is open in the subspace topology). The space $X=\Gamma\backslash H$ consists of the cosets $\Gamma h=\{\gamma h:\gamma\in\Gamma\}$ and has the quotient topology. It is locally compact Hausdorff. Right multiplication defines the right action $X\times H\to X$, $(x,a)\mapsto xa$, where $(\Gamma h)a=\Gamma(ha)$, $xe=x$ for the identity $e$ of $H$, and $(xa)b=x(ab)$ for all $x\in X$ and $a,b\in H$. This map is continuous for the product topology on $X\times H$. There exists a nonzero right-invariant Radon measure $\mu$ on $X$, and every such measure is positive on each nonempty open subset. Right invariance means $\mu(Eh)=\mu(E)$ for every Borel set $E$ and every $h\in H$, where $Eh=\{xh:x\in E\}$.

### Facts & Assumptions

**Given:** The full group $H$ and rational embedding, the full order $O\subset B$ with its basis used to specify the finite level, and the Axiom of Choice.

[A1] The Axiom of Choice says every indexed family of nonempty sets admits a choice function ([math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice)). It applies to the Haar existence and quotient measure results used below.

[F1] The group $H$ is locally compact Hausdorff, and the diagonal embedding $G(\mathbb Q)\to H$ is a group homomorphism with image $\Gamma$ ([def-author-5b8600b4f4af-full-adelic-hamilton-group](item-def-author-5b8600b4f4af-full-adelic-hamilton-group)). The finite level $K_f=\prod_\ell K_\ell$ is open, and each $K_\ell$ is the projective image of $O_\ell^\times$. Every local order unit has at least one unit coefficient in the order basis ([def-finite-quaternion-adeles-and-projective-units](item-def-finite-quaternion-adeles-and-projective-units)). The order $O_\ell$ consists of quaternions with order-basis coefficients in $\mathbb Z_\ell$, the unit ball in $\mathbb Q_\ell$, and a coefficient in $\mathbb Z_\ell$ is a unit exactly when its absolute value is $1$. The rational Hamilton norm is the sum of four squares and is multiplicative. The inverse of a nonzero rational quaternion $x$ is $\bar x/N(x)$ ([def-author-6f52147f3ab3-hamilton-order-completions](item-def-author-6f52147f3ab3-hamilton-order-completions)).

[F2] Every prime $\ell$ satisfies $\ell>1$ ([math:def-prime](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-prime)). An integer divides another when the latter is an integer multiple of the former. A positive divisor of a positive integer is at most that integer ([math:def-divides-in-z](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-divides-in-z), [math:lem-divisor-bound](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-divisor-bound)). Write rationals as fractions of integers with nonzero denominator, with equality tested by cross multiplication ([math:def-rationals](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-rationals)). The gcd of a pair of integers divides both entries and is divisible by every common divisor. It is positive if some entry is nonzero, and the gcd of $(0,0)$ is $0$ ([math:def-common-divisor-and-gcd](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-common-divisor-and-gcd), [math:cor-common-divisor-divides-gcd](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/cor-common-divisor-divides-gcd)). For nonzero rational $a/b$, its $\ell$-adic absolute value is $\ell^{-v_\ell(a/b)}$, where $v_\ell(a/b)$ is the exponent of $\ell$ in $a$ minus its exponent in $b$ ([math:def-p-adic-absolute-value-on-the-rationals](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-p-adic-absolute-value-on-the-rationals), [math:lem-p-adic-valuation-on-q](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-p-adic-valuation-on-q)). Prime factorization supplies a prime divisor of any integer greater than one ([math:thm-fundamental-theorem-of-arithmetic](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-fundamental-theorem-of-arithmetic)). The rational absolute value extends to a multiplicative absolute value on $\mathbb Q_\ell$ ([math:thm-p-adic-completion-is-a-field](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-p-adic-completion-is-a-field)).

[F3] A subgroup contains the identity and is closed under multiplication and inverses ([math:def-subgroup](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-subgroup)). A quotient topology tests openness by inverse image. Cosets $\Gamma h$ depend only on the representative modulo left multiplication by $\Gamma$. Topological group multiplication and inversion are continuous, and translations are homeomorphisms since the inverse of translation by an element is translation by its inverse ([math:def-quotient-topology](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-quotient-topology), [math:def-coset](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-coset), [math:def-topological-group](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-topological-group)). A map is continuous exactly when open sets have open preimages. An open map sends open sets to open sets. A homeomorphism is a continuous bijection with continuous inverse ([math:def-continuous-map-top](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-continuous-map-top), [math:def-homeomorphism-and-open-maps](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-homeomorphism-and-open-maps)). For a closed subgroup $L$ of a locally compact Hausdorff group $J$, the space $J/L$ of cosets $jL$ is locally compact Hausdorff under the choice hypothesis ([Compact lifts and averaging onto $C_c(G/H)$](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-closed-subgroup-quotient-averaging-and-compact-lifts)).

[F4] A left Haar measure is a nonzero left-invariant Radon measure on a locally compact Hausdorff group and exists under [A1] ([math:cor-existence-of-left-and-right-haar-measures](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/cor-existence-of-left-and-right-haar-measures)). The modular function $\Delta_J(j)$ is the positive factor by which right translation by $j^{-1}$ scales the left Haar integral. It is a homomorphism to the multiplicative group of positive reals ([math:def-modular-function-of-a-locally-compact-group](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-modular-function-of-a-locally-compact-group), [math:thm-the-modular-function-is-a-continuous-homomorphism](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-the-modular-function-is-a-continuous-homomorphism)). Under [A1], if $L$ is closed in $J$ and $\Delta_J|_L=\Delta_L$, then $J/L$ has a nonzero left $J$-invariant Radon measure ([math:prop-invariant-measure-on-g-mod-h-iff-modular-functions-agree](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/prop-invariant-measure-on-g-mod-h-iff-modular-functions-agree)).

[F5] The Borel sigma-algebra is the smallest sigma-algebra containing the open sets. A sigma-algebra contains the whole space and is closed under complements and countable unions ([math:def-borel-sigma-algebra](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-borel-sigma-algebra), [math:def-sigma-algebra](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-sigma-algebra)). A measure gives the empty set mass zero and is countably additive on pairwise disjoint measurable sets ([math:def-measure](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-measure)). A Radon measure is a Borel measure finite on compact sets, outer regular on Borel sets, and inner regular on open sets. In particular the mass of an open set is the supremum of the masses of its compact subsets ([math:def-radon-measure-on-an-lch-space](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-radon-measure-on-an-lch-space)).

[F6] A set is closed when its complement is open. Arbitrary unions and finite intersections of open sets are open ([math:def-topological-space](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-topological-space)). Subspace opens are traces of ambient open sets ([math:def-subspace-topology-top](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-subspace-topology-top)). In a Hausdorff space distinct points have disjoint open neighborhoods, so singletons are closed ([math:def-hausdorff-space](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-hausdorff-space)). Local compactness means every point has a compact neighborhood. Every ambient open cover of a compact subset has a finite subcover, and continuous images of compact subsets are compact ([math:def-locally-compact-space](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-locally-compact-space), [math:def-compact-space](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-compact-space), [math:lem-compactness-of-a-subspace-is-ambient](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-compactness-of-a-subspace-is-ambient), [math:thm-compactness-under-continuous-maps](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-compactness-under-continuous-maps)). Products of open sets in two factor spaces form a basis of their product topology ([math:def-product-topology](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-product-topology)).

[F7] A measure is monotone under inclusion of measurable sets and finitely subadditive on unions of measurable sets ([math:prop-measure-monotonicity](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/prop-measure-monotonicity), [math:thm-finite-and-countable-subadditivity-of-measures](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-finite-and-countable-subadditivity-of-measures)).

[F8] A product of four finite sets is finite by the finite product rule ([math:thm-product-rule](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-product-rule)).

### Proof

**Goal:** Verify discreteness and the modular equality, and construct the measure and action on the indicated coset space.

1.1 Suppose the finite component of $\gamma\in\Gamma$ belongs to $K_f$. Clear denominators in the order-basis coefficients of a rational representative to get an integral tuple $(n_0,n_1,n_2,n_3)$ with at least one nonzero entry. Set $d=\gcd(\gcd(n_0,n_1),\gcd(n_2,n_3))$. At least one inner gcd is positive, so $d>0$. The binary gcd properties applied to the two pairs and then to their gcds show that $d$ divides each $n_r$ and that every common divisor of all four entries divides $d$. Divide the representative by $d$ to obtain $x$ with integral coefficients $n_r/d$. If some prime $\ell$ divided every $n_r/d$, then $\ell d$ would be a common divisor of all four $n_r$ and would divide $d$. The positive divisor bound would give $\ell d\le d$, contrary to $\ell d>d$ because $\ell>1$ and $d>0$. Thus for every prime $\ell$ at least one coefficient of $x$ is not divisible by $\ell$. The rational valuation formula gives that coefficient absolute value $1$ and all integral coefficients absolute value at most $1$, so the maximum is $1$. [F1, F2]

1.2 The diagonal embedding sends the rational identity to $e$. If $\gamma_1$ and $\gamma_2$ are the images of $r_1$ and $r_2$ in $G(\mathbb Q)$, then $\gamma_1\gamma_2$ is the image of $r_1r_2$ and $\gamma_1^{-1}$ is the image of $r_1^{-1}$ by the componentwise group operations. Thus $\Gamma$ is a subgroup of $H$. Right multiplication is well defined on $X$ because changing $h$ to $\gamma h$ changes $ha$ to $\gamma ha$. The identities $xe=x$ and $(xa)b=x(ab)$ follow from the identity and associativity in $H$. The quotient map $p:H\to X$ is open because $p^{-1}(p(U))=\bigcup_{\gamma\in\Gamma}\gamma U$ for open $U\subseteq H$. The map $p\times\mathrm{id}_H$ sends every basic open rectangle $U\times V$ to the open rectangle $p(U)\times V$. Its inverse image of an open rectangle $A\times V$ is $p^{-1}(A)\times V$, which is open since $p$ is continuous. Thus $p\times\mathrm{id}_H$ is a continuous open surjection, so the codomain has its quotient topology. The composite of the action with this product map sends $(h,a)$ to $p(ha)$ and is continuous. The quotient topology test then makes the action continuous. For a fixed $a\in H$, the map $x\mapsto (x,a)$ is continuous by the rectangle basis, so $x\mapsto xa$ is continuous. Its inverse is right translation by $a^{-1}$, which is continuous for the same reason. Thus each right translation is a homeomorphism. [F1, F3, F6]

2.1 At each prime $\ell$, write $x=a_\ell u_\ell$ with $a_\ell\in\mathbb Q_\ell^\times$ and $u_\ell\in O_\ell^\times$, which is possible by the definition of $K_\ell$. The maximum of the absolute values of the coefficients of $u_\ell$ is $1$ by its unit coefficient. By multiplicativity on $\mathbb Q_\ell$, the maximum for $x$ is $|a_\ell|_\ell$ times the maximum for $u_\ell$. Hence $|a_\ell|_\ell=1$ and $|a_\ell^{-1}|_\ell=1$. This gives $a_\ell^{-1}\in\mathbb Z_\ell$ and $x^{-1}=a_\ell^{-1}u_\ell^{-1}\in O_\ell$. Every rational order-basis coefficient of $x^{-1}$ now has absolute value at most $1$ at every prime. Zero coefficients are integral. For a nonzero rational coefficient write $m/n$ with $n>0$, and set $g=\gcd(m,n)>0$. Replacing the numerator and denominator by $m'=m/g$ and $n'=n/g$ gives the same fraction by cross multiplication. If a prime $\ell$ divided both $m'$ and $n'$, then $\ell g$ would be a common divisor of $m,n$ and would divide $g$, giving $\ell g\le g$ by the positive divisor bound. But $\ell>1$ and $g>0$ give $\ell g>g$. If the coefficient is nonintegral, $n'>1$. A prime divisor $\ell$ of $n'$ then has positive exponent in $n'$ and zero exponent in $m'$, so $v_\ell(m'/n')<0$. Since $\ell>1$, the absolute value $\ell^{-v_\ell(m'/n')}$ is greater than $1$. Therefore $x,x^{-1}\in O$. [F1, F2, step 1.1]

3.1 There is a fixed integer $D\ge1$ clearing all denominators of the coefficients of the norm polynomial in the order basis. Thus $N(x)$ and $N(x^{-1})$ are positive multiples of $1/D$. Their product is $1$, so $N(x)\le D$. Each Hamilton coordinate has square at most $D$ and absolute value at most $D$. Write each entry of the fixed matrix from Hamilton to order-basis coordinates as $m_{rs}/n_{rs}$ with $m_{rs}\in\mathbb Z$ and $n_{rs}\ge1$. Since $n_{rs}\ge1$ and the Hamilton coordinate $x_s$ lies between $-D$ and $D$, the summand $(m_{rs}/n_{rs})x_s$ lies between $-D|m_{rs}|$ and $D|m_{rs}|$. Adding the four bounds shows that the $r$-th order-basis coordinate has absolute value at most $D\sum_{s=0}^3|m_{rs}|$. Thus $M=D(1+\sum_{r,s=0}^3|m_{rs}|)$ is a common integer bound. Each order-basis coordinate belongs to the finite set $S=\{-M,-M+1,\ldots,M\}$, enumerated by $j\mapsto j-M$ for $j=0,\ldots,2M$. The product rule makes $S^4$ finite, and each tuple in it represents at most one projective class. Thus $\Gamma\cap(G_\infty\times K_f)$ is finite. [F1, F2, F8, step 2.1]

4.1 Remove the finitely many nonidentity points of this intersection from $G_\infty\times K_f$. Since singletons are closed in $H$, the result is an open identity neighborhood $V$ with $V\cap\Gamma=\{e\}$. This trace is open in $\Gamma$, and translation makes every singleton of $\Gamma$ open in $\Gamma$. Continuity of $(r,s)\mapsto r^{-1}s$ gives open identity neighborhoods $W_1,W_2$ with $W_1^{-1}W_2\subseteq V$. Put $W=W_1\cap W_2$. If $\gamma_1,\gamma_2\in hW\cap\Gamma$, closure under inverses and multiplication puts $\gamma_1^{-1}\gamma_2$ in $\Gamma$. Writing $\gamma_i=hw_i$ with $w_i\in W$ shows $\gamma_1^{-1}\gamma_2=w_1^{-1}w_2\in W^{-1}W\subseteq V$. Hence $\gamma_1^{-1}\gamma_2\in V\cap\Gamma=\{e\}$, so $\gamma_1=\gamma_2$. Each $hW$ thus contains at most one point of $\Gamma$. For any $h\in H\setminus\Gamma$, the open neighborhood $hW$ is disjoint from $\Gamma$ or meets it at one point $\gamma\ne h$. In the latter case $hW\setminus\{\gamma\}$ is open because the singleton is closed. In either case $h$ has an open neighborhood in $H\setminus\Gamma$. The complement is therefore open as a union of such neighborhoods, so $\Gamma$ is closed and discrete. [F1, F3, F6, step 3.1]

5.1 Each element of $\Gamma$ is conjugate within $\Gamma$ to its inverse. Indeed write a rational representative as $x=x_0+z$ with $z=ai+bj+ck$ pure imaginary. If $z=0$ the class is the identity. Otherwise choose $y=bi-aj$ when $(a,b)\ne(0,0)$ and $y=i$ when $(a,b)=(0,0)$. In both cases $y\ne0$ and the Hamilton multiplication rules give $yz+zy=0$. Since $N(y)>0$, this yields $yxy^{-1}=x_0-z=\bar x=N(x)x^{-1}$. The scalar factor disappears in the projective class. The discrete topology on $\Gamma$ is Hausdorff and both its inversion and its multiplication are continuous, since $\Gamma$ and $\Gamma\times\Gamma$ are discrete. Each singleton is an open compact neighborhood of its point, so $\Gamma$ is locally compact. Both $H$ and $\Gamma$ therefore have modular functions under [A1]. For either modular function $f$, multiplicativity gives $f(e)=f(e)^2$, hence $f(e)=1$ by positivity. Then $f(g)f(g^{-1})=f(e)=1$, so $f(g^{-1})=f(g)^{-1}$. Multiplicativity and commutativity of real multiplication give $f(ugu^{-1})=f(u)f(g)f(u)^{-1}=f(g)$. For $\gamma\in\Gamma$, the conjugacy just proved applies in both groups, giving $f(\gamma)=f(\gamma^{-1})=f(\gamma)^{-1}$. Positivity gives $f(\gamma)=1$. Thus $\Delta_H(\gamma)=\Delta_\Gamma(\gamma)=1$ for every $\gamma\in\Gamma$. [A1, F1, F4, F6, step 4.1]

6.1 The closed-subgroup result makes $H/\Gamma$ locally compact Hausdorff. The modular equality gives a nonzero left-invariant Radon measure $\lambda$ there. Inversion on $H$ descends to the bijection $\phi(h\Gamma)=\Gamma h^{-1}$, because replacing $h$ by $h\gamma$ changes the inverse to $\gamma^{-1}h^{-1}$. The two coset topologies are quotient topologies, so inversion on $H$ induces continuous maps $\phi$ and $\phi^{-1}$ in the two directions. The homeomorphism carries compact neighborhoods and disjoint open neighborhoods from $H/\Gamma$ to $X$, so $X$ is locally compact Hausdorff. [A1, F3, F4, F6, step 4.1, step 5.1]

7.1 For any continuous map between topological spaces, the sets whose preimages are Borel form a sigma-algebra because preimages commute with complements and countable unions and take the whole codomain to the whole domain. It contains all open sets, so preimages of Borel sets are Borel. Applying this in both directions, the homeomorphism $\phi$ bijects the Borel sets of $H/\Gamma$ and $X$. Define $\mu(E)=\lambda(\phi^{-1}(E))$ for Borel $E\subseteq X$. The empty set has mass zero. Preimages of a pairwise disjoint sequence are pairwise disjoint, and the preimage of its union is their union, so countable additivity of $\lambda$ gives countable additivity of $\mu$. Also $\mu(X)=\lambda(H/\Gamma)>0$ since $\lambda$ is nonzero. Open supersets of $E$ correspond under $\phi^{-1}$ to open supersets of its preimage, and compact subsets of an open set correspond to compact subsets of its preimage. Finite mass on compact sets, outer regularity on Borel sets and inner regularity on open sets thus follow from the same properties of $\lambda$. [F3, F5, F6, F7, step 6.1]

8.1 For Borel $E\subseteq X$ and $a\in H$, the translate $Ea$ is Borel because it is the preimage of $E$ under the continuous right translation by $a^{-1}$. The identity $\phi(a^{-1}h\Gamma)=\phi(h\Gamma)a$ gives $\phi^{-1}(Ea)=a^{-1}\phi^{-1}(E)$. By left invariance of $\lambda$, this yields $\mu(Ea)=\lambda(a^{-1}\phi^{-1}(E))=\lambda(\phi^{-1}(E))=\mu(E)$. [F4, F5, step 6.1, step 7.1, step 1.2]

9.1 Let $\mu$ be any nonzero right-invariant Radon measure on $X$ and $U$ any nonempty open set. Right multiplication is transitive because $h^{-1}h'$ sends $\Gamma h$ to $\Gamma h'$. The open right translates of $U$ therefore cover $X$. For a compact subset $K$, fix any point $z$ outside $K$. Open neighborhoods of points of $K$ disjoint from some open neighborhood of $z$ cover $K$ by Hausdorff separation. Take a finite subcover and intersect the corresponding open neighborhoods of $z$ to obtain an open neighborhood of $z$ disjoint from $K$. Thus $K$ is closed and Borel. Choose finitely many translates $Uh_i$ covering $K$. Monotonicity and finite subadditivity give $\mu(K)\le\mu(\bigcup_i Uh_i)\le\sum_i\mu(Uh_i)$. If $\mu(U)=0$, right invariance makes the sum zero for every such cover. Inner regularity on the open set $X$ then gives $\mu(X)=0$, and monotonicity forces $\mu$ to be the zero measure. Thus $\mu(U)>0$. [F5, F6, F7, step 6.1, step 1.2, step 8.1] ∎


## Unramified representations at split quaternion primes

[Open item](item-def-author-5b8600b4f4af-split-unramified-quaternion-representation) · level 5


### Definition

Assume the Axiom of Choice for compactness of the integral subgroup. A finite prime $\ell$ is split for the rational Hamilton algebra $B$ when its local algebra $B_\ell$ admits a $\mathbb Q_\ell$-algebra isomorphism $\phi:B_\ell\to M_2(\mathbb Q_\ell)$. For a field $F$, the two-by-two matrix algebra $M_2(F)$ consists of matrices with two rows, two columns, and entries in $F$. It has entrywise addition and row-by-column multiplication, with identity $I_2$ and scalar map $a\mapsto aI_2$. The isomorphism is a unital ring isomorphism that respects the scalar maps $a\mapsto a1$ in $B_\ell$ and $a\mapsto aI_2$ in the matrix algebra.
