4.2 Transport the irreducible unitary local factor from step 3.1 before scalar descent along the split isomorphism used for $J_\ell$. The isomorphism and its inverse are continuous by the finite-coordinate argument in step 1.2, so the transported action remains strongly continuous by [F2]. By [F8] the resulting acting group is the $\mathbb Q_\ell$-points of the general linear algebraic group of degree two, which is reductive by Milne’s example in [F6]. The embedding in [F8] is continuous for the matrix-entry topology by [F15], since the determinant is polynomial and inversion on $\mathbb Q_\ell^\times$ is continuous. Projection onto the matrix entries gives its continuous inverse on the image. Thus the local-points topology agrees with the matrix-entry topology for which the action is strongly continuous. The field $\mathbb Q_\ell$ is nonarchimedean and locally compact by [F8] and nondiscrete since the distinct nonzero powers of $\ell$ converge to zero. The smooth-vector and admissibility results in [F6] therefore apply. For $n\ge1$, entrywise reduction of an integral matrix modulo $\ell^n$ gives $I$ exactly on $I+\ell^n M_2(\mathbb Z_\ell)$ by [F10]. For $A=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\c&d\end{pmatrix}$ in this set, $ad-bc$ reduces to $1$ modulo $\ell$, so it is a unit of $\mathbb Z_\ell$ by [F10]. Multiplying $A$ on either side by $(ad-bc)^{-1}\begin{pmatrix}d&-b\\-c&a\end{pmatrix}$ gives $I$. Thus these sets are the kernels of the reduction homomorphisms on $\mathrm{GL}_2(\mathbb Z_\ell)$. Each kernel is a product of four translates of $\ell^n\mathbb Z_\ell$. Multiplication by $\ell^n$ and translation are homeomorphisms of $\mathbb Q_\ell$ by [F15], with inverses multiplication by $\ell^{-n}$ and translation by the opposite constant, respectively. Hence these translates are compact open by [F8]. The inherited matrix topology on the kernel is this product topology, so [F12] makes it compact. Each kernel is open in $\mathrm{GL}_2(\mathbb Q_\ell)$, and these kernels form a neighborhood basis at $I$ by [F10] and the product basis of the matrix coordinate topology. Every open subgroup therefore contains a compact open subgroup from this basis. Conversely, each compact open subgroup is open, so the source’s smooth vectors are exactly those fixed by some open subgroup. The lift of the projective local factor to the same matrix group has this action and meets the hypothesis of [F5]. Whenever the local factor has $J_\ell$-fixed vectors, [F5] gives the claimed line and character. [A1, F2, F5, F6, F8, F10, F12, F15, step 1.2, step 3.1]

5.1 Work in the full restricted Hilbert tensor product supplied by [F6] and realized projectively in step 4.1. Let $V_\ell$ be the factor at $\ell$ and choose a unit vector $e\in V_\ell$, using the reference vector there if one is prescribed. Let $W$ be the closed linear span in the full product of elementary tensors with $\ell$-coordinate $e$. This is a Hilbert space by [F4]: each Cauchy sequence converges in the full product and its limit lies in the closed subspace. For each elementary tensor $x$ in the full product with $\ell$-coordinate $u$, let $w_x\in W$ be the tensor obtained by replacing $u$ by $e$. For two such elementary tensors with coordinates $u,u'$, the inner product of $w_x,w_{x'}$ is the product of the pairings over the places other than $\ell$, because the omitted pairing has been replaced by $\langle e,e\rangle=1$. Thus $x\mapsto u\otimes w_x$ preserves pairings by the binary tensor construction in [F4]. By sesquilinearity it preserves the norms of finite sums, so a sum representing zero goes to zero and the rule defines a linear isometry $T$ on the original restricted elementary span. The range is dense in $V_\ell\widehat{\otimes}W$. Indeed, for any $u\otimes w$, choose finite sums $w_n$ from the defining span of $W$ with $\|w-w_n\|<1/n$. The regrouped images contain each $u\otimes w_n$ by replacing $e$ by $u$ in each summand, and their error has norm $\|u\|\,\|w-w_n\|$ by [F4]. Finite sums of binary elementary tensors are dense by [F4]. By [A1] and [F14], $T$ extends to a bounded linear map $\widehat T$ from the original tensor completion into the binary Hilbert product. Norm equality passes to the limit along a sequence from the dense domain of $T$, so $\widehat T$ is a linear isometry. Its range is closed because every convergent sequence of images has Cauchy preimages, which converge in the complete domain. The range contains the dense range of $T$, so $\widehat T$ is surjective. [A1, F4, F6, F14, step 4.1, choose]

6.1 Under the coordinate identification in [F11], the copy of $J_\ell$ has identity in every other coordinate. By step 4.1 and the regrouping in step 5.1, it acts on the $\ell$-coordinate of each elementary tensor and leaves the other coordinates fixed. In the binary model it therefore acts through the first factor alone. If $U$ contains this copy, then $M_i^K\subseteq M_i^{J_\ell}$. By [F4], a nonzero $M_i^{J_\ell}$ requires a nonzero local fixed space, and the finite coset sum of [F5] acts on $M_i^{J_\ell}$ by the scalar character from step 4.2. It therefore preserves $M_i^K$ and acts there by that same scalar. This gives the stated unramified action on all contributing summands. [A1, F2, F4, F5, F11, step 2.2, step 4.1, step 4.2, step 5.1] ∎
